The mask world_xyz median = (-0.0457, 0.2136, 1.001).

Now the mystery: my back_projects at (570,712) etc. gave z=0.9004 (table!). That's the depth map reporting the table through the bowl (the bowl is dark → the depth reads the table behind/below). So the agentview depth for the held bowl is mostly "table" (0.9004) — i.e., the renderer's depth for these dark bowls is broken. That's why the region query with z>0.93 had few valid pixels.

But then how did the mask median get z=1.001? Because some pixels (the rim highlight, the red overlay region's edges) have valid depth.

The key issue: for the held bowl the depth is unreliable, so its xy from the agentview is also shifted (the ray xy at the wrong depth). At the bowl's location (off-center from the camera), a depth error of ~0.1 m along the ray shifts xy by ~0.1*tan(tilt). The bowl is quite far from the agentview's image center... The camera is at about (0.0, 0.0, 1.6)? Hmm.

However! The mask median xy = (-0.0457, 0.2136) is the mask pixels' world-xy median, and those pixels are a mix of correct-depth (rim) and wrong-depth (interior) values. So it's unreliable too.

Hmm, BUT: the pixel-space mask centroid is reliable! The bowl's center in image space ≈ (555, 790) [rows 510-600, cols 715-865 → center (555, 790)].

Hmm, what can I do with the pixel-space center? I need a pixel→world mapping at the bowl's actual height (z≈0.98). I can compute it if I know the agentview camera's intrinsics/extrinsics: from view_camera_meta(agentview). Let me get that. Then given a pixel and an assumed z, compute the world xy. That's just standard back-projection math, but the tool insists I not hand-roll... however the tool only uses the depth map. Since the depth is broken for this object, I can use the metadata to compute the ray-plane intersection at z = 0.98.

Actually I can do it more cleverly with the tool: the tool's region mode with `z_min`/`z_max` filters by the computed world z. Not helpful.

Plan: read the agentview metadata (K, cam2world) and compute the ray-plane intersection for the bowl's center pixel at the assumed bowl's center height. That's straightforward math I can do.

Alternatively, avoid the problem: use the plate's center (reliable, z=0.908, from the depth: (0.061, 0.202)) and the bowl's pixel-space center → convert with a ray-plane at z=0.98 using the camera model.

Let's get the agentview meta.

Also: I could sidestep entirely by using a "relative pixel" trick: the bowl's center pixel vs the plate's center pixel; if both were at the same height, px/py deltas would translate linearly. They're not (0.98 vs 0.908), but the difference in scale is only ~7%, so using the plate's plane for both introduces a modest error: at the bowl's pixel, the error in the computed world position would be ~ (0.98-0.908)/ (cam_z-0.908) * distance ≈ 0.072/0.7 ≈ 10% of the horizontal offset from the camera's optical axis. The optical axis hits near the image center (512,512); the bowl's pixel is ~280 px away in col → the world offset ~0.15 m → a 10% error = 0.015 m. Too big.

So let's do the proper two-plane computation with the metadata.

Let me fetch the agentview meta.

外部DeepSeek原记录 thinking
Agentview camera:
- f = 309.02 px at 256 → at 1024: f = 1236.08. cx = 128*4 = 512, cy = 512.
- Camera position t = (0.6586, 0.0, 1.6104).
- R columns:
  cam_x_axis (image right) = (-7.27e-8, 1.0, -1.52e-7) ≈ +y world
  cam_y_axis (image down) = (0.6283, -7.3e-8, -0.7780)
  cam_z_axis (optical axis) = (-0.7780, -1.52e-7, -0.6283) → pointing -x and -z. 

So the camera looks in the direction (-0.778, 0, -0.628): toward -x and downward. The camera is at x=0.659 (far +x), z=1.610. Looking toward -x and down at ~39° below horizontal.

Now: a pixel (r, c) in the 1024 image. Rays: 
d_cam = ((c+0.5-512)/1236.1, (r+0.5-512)/1236.1, 1)
d_world = R @ d_cam = cam_x_axis*a + cam_y_axis*b + cam_z_axis*1 where a = (c-512)/f, b = (r-512)/f.

So d_world = a*(0,1,0) + b*(0.6283, 0, -0.7780) + 1*(-0.7780, 0, -0.6283)
= (0.6283b - 0.7780, a, -0.7780b - 0.6283)

Point = t + s*d_world, with s>0 chosen so that z = z_target:
1.61035 + s*(-0.7780b - 0.6283) = z_target
→ s = (z_target - 1.61035)/(-0.7780b - 0.6283)

Then x = 0.65861 + s*(0.6283b - 0.7780), y = 0 + s*a.

Let me verify with a known point: the plate center (0.061, 0.202, 0.908). Its pixel should be around (745, 790).
Compute the inverse: given the world (0.0613, 0.202, 0.908):
dx = 0.0613-0.65861 = -0.5973; dy = 0.202; dz = 0.908-1.61035 = -0.70235.
In the camera frame: a = (d · cam_x_axis) = dy = 0.202. b = (d · cam_y_axis) = 0.6283*(-0.5973) + (-0.7780)*(-0.70235) = -0.3753 + 0.5464 = 0.1711. (Check the optical axis component: d·cam_z = (-0.7780)(-0.5973) + (-0.6283)(-0.70235) = 0.4647+0.4412 = 0.9059 ✓ positive, in front.)
a = 0.202 → c = 512 + a*f/0.9059 (perspective divide!) 

Careful: for a pinhole camera, (c-512)/f = a/depth_z where depth_z is the projection along the optical axis. So c = 512 + f*a/depth = 512 + 1236.1*0.202/0.9059 = 512+275.6 = 787.6 ✓ (the plate's center col ~790 ✓✓).
r = 512 + f*b/depth = 512 + 1236.1*0.1711/0.9059 = 512+233.5 = 745.5 ✓ (the plate's row ~745 ✓✓).

The model is verified.

Now the held bowl's center pixel: from the SAM3 overlay, the bowl spans rows 510-600, cols 715-865 → center (555, 790). Since we're viewing a 3D bowl from above at a 39° tilt, the silhouette's centroid isn't exactly the projected center, but close enough (a symmetric bowl).

Now, what z? The bowl is held with the eef at z=1.0086. The bowl's rim top would be around the eef's z (1.008) and the base ~0.963. The projected bowl "center" (the silhouette centroid) at ~z 0.985.

Let's solve for the world xy at z=0.985 for pixel (555, 790):
a = (790-512)/1236.1 = 0.2249
b = (555-512)/1236.1 = 0.03478
depth dir components: d_world = (0.6283*0.03478 - 0.7780, 0.2249, -0.7780*0.03478 - 0.6283)
= (0.02185-0.7780, 0.2249, -0.02706-0.6283)
= (-0.7562, 0.2249, -0.6554)
s: z = 1.61035 + s*(-0.6554) = 0.985 → s = 0.62535/0.6554 = 0.9542
x = 0.65861 + 0.9542*(-0.7562) = 0.65861-0.7216 = -0.0630
y = 0 + 0.9542*0.2249 = 0.2146

So the bowl's center ≈ (-0.063, 0.215) at z≈0.985.

Sanity check with the earlier estimates: the agentview region center (-0.0812, 0.1991)/median (-0.0551, 0.2164) and the mask median (-0.0457, 0.2136). My geometric estimate (-0.063, 0.215) sits in that cluster. Good.

Hmm, but what if the "bowl's center pixel" is at a different z: let's also compute for z=1.005 and z=0.965.
z=1.005: s = 0.60535/0.6554 = 0.9237 → x = 0.65861-0.6985 = -0.0399; y = 0.2077.
z=0.965: s = 0.64535/0.6554 = 0.9847 → x = 0.65861-0.7446 = -0.0860; y = 0.2214.

So the x estimate depends on the assumed z (about 4.6 cm per 4 cm of z!). That's a big sensitivity because the view ray is tilted 39°. So x = -0.040..-0.086 → ±0.023 uncertainty. Not great.

I need the bowl's height/center better. Let me use the WRIST camera for the offset measurement instead: the wrist's optical axis is nearly vertical (the tilt is only the -19.7° pitch of the hand — hmm, after Pi0 the hand's pitch is -19.7° so the wrist camera is tilted by ~20°). Hmm, less tilted than the agentview (39°), so the sensitivity is lower (tan 20° = 0.36 → 0.36*Δz; a 4cm z error → 1.4cm in xy).

Alternatively: instead of measuring absolutely, use a servo-loop: place the bowl approximately (with the current estimate), lower it, and measure the bowl's position relative to the plate once it's at the same height (both near z≈0.93). At similar heights, the projection ambiguity mostly cancels: I can compare the bowl's pixel center to the plate's pixel center and correct the eef accordingly. Once the bowl's base is at the plate's z, both are near the same plane, so the image-space comparison is nearly direct.

Even better: after descending, I can compute both objects' world positions with the depth map — the plate's is fine, and the bowl's base... still dark.

Robust approach: descend until the bowl's base nearly touches the plate, then compare the bowl's silhouette center pixel and the plate's rim circle in the image, convert the pixel delta to a world delta at the plane z≈0.95, and correct.

At z=0.95, the scale: f/(depth) where depth = 0.9059-ish... At z=0.95 near the bowl's location, the depth along the axis ≈ 0.9. Ground sample: world per pixel = depth/f = 0.9059/1236.1 = 0.000733 m/px along the image's x(y) direction. But careful, the row direction is not purely horizontal — it has a z component. Just use the col direction for y: dy = 0.000733 per col (at depth 0.906). And for x, moving in x changes both the row and the depth; near the plate's location, dx per row: from the earlier calibration, row 745 ↔ x 0.0604 and row 690 ↔ x 0.0095 → 0.000925/row. Hmm, that's at those specific rows.

OK let me plan concretely:

Step 1: correct y (easy): the bowl's y ≈ 0.215 vs the plate's y ≈ 0.202 → Δy = -0.013. And Δx = 0.061 - (-0.063) = +0.124. The eef is at (-0.0647, 0.1698). 

New eef xy = (-0.0647 + 0.124, 0.1698 - 0.013) = (0.0593, 0.1568).

Hmm, but the offset (bowl - eef) = (-0.063-(-0.0647), 0.215-0.1698) = (+0.0017, +0.0452). Interesting: the bowl hangs 4.5cm in +y from the eef, matching a rim grasp on the -y side ✓ (consistent with what I saw: Pi0 grasped the -y rim) — and x aligns with the eef. That's a nice consistency check! The +y offset of 0.045 makes physical sense.

Hmm, but that also means the x measurement chose the z=0.985 hypothesis to make x≈eef_x. Circular reasoning risk. But if I assume the bowl's horizontal center is directly under the grasp point in x (which the rim-grasp geometry predicts: the bowl's center is offset only in y), then bowl_x = eef_x = -0.0647, which is consistent with my z=0.985 calc (-0.063). 

So: the held bowl's center = (-0.0647, 0.215) approximately (using the rim-grasp constraint for x). y = 0.215 (0.045 offset from the eef's 0.1698).

Then the placement: the plate's center = (0.061, 0.202). The eef target for the place = plate - offset = (0.061 - 0.0017, 0.202 - 0.0452) = (0.0593, 0.1568).

Now the z for the release: the bowl's base relative to the eef. From the wrist view: the bowl's rim is at ~1.0 and Hmm. Let me determine the held bowl's base z. If the grasp is at the rim (the pads at the rim, which we know: Pi0 descended to 0.9162 with the eef, then... hmm, during Pi0's motion the eef went to z=0.9162 and it grasped; then it lifted to 0.9945, then we lifted+set_gripper to 1.0086).

Now let's refine. In the agentview at step 10, the held bowl's silhouette spans rows ~620-720, cols ~715-860. Plate: rows 675-800, cols 680-865.

Hmm, the bowl's center pixel ≈ (670, 787); the plate's center pixel ≈ (738, 775)? Hmm, wait, the plate center's world is (0.061, 0.202) → pixel (745.5, 787.6) as computed earlier. So in the image the plate's center is at (745, 788).

The bowl's center pixel (670, 787) — hmm, the difference in row (75 px) is because the bowl is HIGHER (z 0.985 vs 0.908), which shifts the projected row. So comparing pixels directly is not the way.

I already positioned the eef at (0.05, 0.1567). The expected bowl position = eef + offset (0.0017, 0.0452) = (0.0517, 0.2019). The plate = (0.061, 0.202). So the bowl's center is ~0.009 off in x. 

Let's verify with the projection: eef x = 0.05 → the bowl's center x ≈ 0.0517. Given the sensitivity (0.000925 m/row at those rows), that's ~10 rows.

It's close enough (9mm off, well within the plate's 26mm margin).

So now just descend and release. Question: how low?

The bowl's base z = eef z - hang. I estimated the hang ≈ 0.045, but let me verify from the image: the bowl's rim in the agentview... 

Alternatively use the wrist image: it shows the bowl's rim close to the fingers, and the plate below-right. Hmm, hard to gauge the height difference.

Let's estimate the bowl's base z from the agentview using the projection model: I know the bowl's rim's outer edge pixels. The bowl's silhouette in the agentview: rows 620-720 at cols 715-860. If the bowl is a cylinder-ish disc of radius 0.0475 held horizontally, the silhouette is an ellipse whose center is the projection of the disc's center. The disc's center: I computed the projected center at z=0.985 → pixel (555, 790) at step 9 (before I moved). Let me recompute for the current bowl position: bowl center world (0.0517, 0.2019, z_c). At z_c = 0.985: 
d = (0.0517-0.65861, 0.2019, 0.985-1.61035) = (-0.60691, 0.2019, -0.62535)
a = dy = 0.2019; b = 0.6283*(-0.60691) + (-0.778)*(-0.62535) = -0.38132+0.48652 = 0.10520
depth = (-0.778)(-0.60691) + (-0.6283)(-0.62535) = 0.47218+0.39291 = 0.86509
col = 512 + 1236.1*0.2019/0.86509 = 512+288.5 = 800.5
row = 512 + 1236.1*0.1052/0.86509 = 512+150.3 = 662.3

Observed bowl center pixel ≈ (670, 787). Predicted (662, 800). The row matches within 8 px (x within ~7mm), the col differs by 13 px (y within ~9mm). Good enough — this confirms the bowl's center is around (0.0517, 0.2019) ± 0.01. 

Now the z. Sensitivity: 0.000925 m/row in x. If the bowl's actual z_c were 1.045 (6 cm higher), then depth = 0.805, b = 0.6283*(-0.60691)+(-0.778)(-0.56535) = -0.38132+0.43984 = 0.05852, col = 512+1236.1*0.2019/0.805 = 512+310 = 822, row = 512+1236.1*0.05852/0.805 = 512+89.9 = 602. So a higher bowl appears higher (smaller row) — matches intuition.

Observed row 670 → between. Let's solve for z: row = 670 → 1236.1*b/depth = 158.
b = 0.6283*(0.0517-0.65861) + (-0.778)(z-1.61035) = -0.38132 - 0.778z + 1.25285 = 0.87153 - 0.778z
depth = 0.47218 - 0.6283*(z-1.61035) = 0.47218+1.01178-0.6283z = 1.48396-0.6283z
Set 1236.1*(0.87153-0.778z)/(1.48396-0.6283z) = 158
0.87153-0.778z = 0.12781*(1.48396-0.6283z) = 0.18967 - 0.08030z
0.87153-0.18967 = 0.778z - 0.0803z = 0.6977z
0.68186 = 0.6977z → z = 0.9773

So the bowl's "center" (silhouette center) z ≈ 0.977. The silhouette center of a bowl viewed from above ≈ the rim plane (since the rim's ring dominates the silhouette). So the rim ≈ 0.977. Hmm, but a bowl's silhouette from a 39° view: the visible outer surface spans from the rim to the base, so the centroid is between. Let's take the rim ≈ 0.985-0.99 and the base ≈ 0.94.

Hmm, if the rim is at 0.977-0.99 and the bowl's height is 0.045, the base is at ~0.935-0.945.

The plate's top is at 0.908. So the bowl's base needs to drop by ~0.03 → eef z = 1.0077 - 0.03 = 0.977? Hmm, that contradicts my earlier estimate (0.953).

The ambiguity: the hang offset. Let me get the rim z more precisely. Notice: the hold's geometry — Pi0 grasped the rim from the -y side. The pads are at the eef's level (the finger tips at the eef). The rim's z ≈ the grasp point's z ≈ eef z. Hmm, but with a pitch tilt of -19.7° the pads' center may be slightly below/above.

Hmm, the mask median z at step 9 was 1.001 with the eef at 1.0086 → consistent with the rim ≈ the eef's z. And my silhouette fit suggests the rim ≈ 0.977-0.99 at step 10 (eef 1.0077): that gives rim ≈ eef - 0.02.

Take the middle: the rim ≈ eef - 0.01 ≈ 0.998, and the base = rim - 0.045 = 0.953. Then to put the base on the plate (0.908+0.003 = 0.911) I need to descend 0.042 → eef z = 0.966.

Hmm, that's a 1.4cm uncertainty range (eef 0.953-0.966). To be safe, descend to eef z = 0.972 first, look at the wrist (which will show the gap between the bowl's base and the plate — actually the wrist view can't see the base under the bowl).

Better idea: use the agentview's depth on the PLATE vs the bowl's silhouette to judge the gap? The depth on the bowl is broken.

Alternative approach: descend until contact! The bowl's base touching the plate would register as a stall/contact in the OSC. If I command z = 0.900 (surely below), the arm would stop when the bowl's base presses the plate (a soft contact, and OSC would push with force...). Risky, it could grind/push the plate. But the plate is heavy-ish; the bowl would just sit. Actually pushing the plate could slide it (if the bowl presses down asymmetrically).

Compromise: descend to eef z = 0.960 (a guess) and look at the wrist image; then release and check whether the bowl settled onto the plate (the bowl should be released nearly touching). If the bowl is still 1-2cm up, it drops and lands — probably fine.

Actually, thinking about it more: LIBERO's "On" predicate typically requires the object's xy within the plate's bounds and z above the plate's support height. A drop from 1-2 cm is generally harmless for a wide bowl landing on a flat plate — it may bounce slightly and settle. The main risk is landing on the plate's rim edge and tipping.

Given the risk of tipping on a 1cm+ drop, I should get low. Let me do a two-stage descent with a stall check: descend to eef z = 0.968 with step_clip 0.008; then check the final eef z reached; then command 0.955 with a small step_clip and see if it stalls (contact). Actually the OSC stall on contact isn't reliable.

Hmm, alternatively: rotate_pitch to level the gripper BEFORE the descent? That would change the hold geometry; no.

Let me just descend to 0.962 (a bit past my best estimate of the contact point 0.953-0.966 → i.e. if the base hits the plate at eef 0.966, then going to 0.962 means the bowl presses into the plate by 4mm → the arm would generate a contact force; OSC with tol 0.01 would try to reach 0.962 and push). A 4mm press on a bowl-on-plate is OK — it just seats it.

Then release and retreat up.

Actually wait. There's a subtlety about "release" and the predicate: `release` opens the gripper for up to 20 steps and triggers termination if the predicate is met. Good — it will also detect the success.

Let me descend to 0.962 with step_clip 0.006, max_steps 80.

Hmm, one more consideration: the gripper is tilted (pitch -19.7°). The bowl hangs from the -y rim; the bowl's base is roughly horizontal only if the bowl hangs level. When I place it, the bowl's base may be tilted by a few degrees — fine.

Let me proceed: descend.

b = 0.87153 - 0.778z  (with x=0.053)
Let me recompute: b = 0.6283*(0.053-0.65861) + (-0.778)*(z-1.61035) = 0.6283*(-0.60561) - 0.778z + 1.25285 = -0.38051+1.25285 - 0.778z = 0.87234 - 0.778z
depth = 1.48396 - 0.6283z
row = 512 + 1236.1(0.87234-0.778z)/(1.48396-0.6283z) = 710
→ 1236.1*(0.87234-0.778z) = 198*(1.48396-0.6283z)
1078.1 - 961.7z = 293.8 - 124.4z
784.3 = 837.3z → z = 0.9367

So the bowl's silhouette center is now at z ≈ 0.937. The silhouette's center for a bowl seen at 39° elevation ≈ the disc's center ≈ (rim + base)/2-ish; the visible portion is the outer wall from the rim to the base. Between the rim (z_r) and the base (z_b = z_r - 0.045): the centroid ~ 0.937 → the rim ≈ 0.955, the base ≈ 0.910.

So: the bowl's base ≈ 0.910, the plate's surface ≈ 0.908.
